# Gameplay Validation Mechanics Audit

## Round 1 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `d306cd8 Unify combat ending player HP reads`.
- Baseline: `D:\anaconda\envs\stsai\python.exe -m pytest -q --basetemp C:\Users\20571\Documents\Codex\2026-06-03\d-pycharmprojects-slay-the-spire-ai\.pytest-basetemp` -> 1453 passed before changes.
- Validation: CommunicationMod ran `scripts/run_training_batch.py --eval --max-games 20 --phase conservative --restart-guidance` with Windows Python.
- Outcome: no Ironclad `victory=true`; AI markers `1780450800` through `1780452924`; latest visible run `1780452921.run` died at floor 16 to Hexaghost.
- Failure type: lethal-sequence execution drift during RL energy-guard fallback takeover. A proven sequence could be planned, but `OptimizedAgent` invalidated the cached sequence after the first card left hand, replanned mid-turn, and could abandon the remaining lethal line.
- Fix: keep executing cached combat sequences when the next planned action is still available, and advance `current_action_index` after returning a newly planned first action.
- Regression: `test_optimized_agent_continues_cached_sequence_after_played_card_leaves_hand`.
- Verification after fix: focused related tests `56 passed`; full pytest `1454 passed`.
- Next candidate: inspect post-fix bounded validation for remaining Act 1 boss deaths and late Act 2 boss deaths, especially low-HP turns where fallback selects zero-damage setup cards or Havoc before lethal/defense.

## Round 2 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `c35d6df Penalize lethal current combat lines`.
- Baseline: full pytest with an isolated `STS_AI_LOG_FILE`, disabled pytest cache, and unique basetemp -> 1455 passed before changes.
- Validation: controlled CommunicationMod restart with Windows Python ran `scripts/run_training_batch.py --eval --max-games 20 --phase conservative --restart-guidance`.
- Outcome before interruption: no Ironclad `victory=true`; new completed runs were `1780455201.run` (floor 19, killed by Shell Parasite) and `1780455267.run` (floor 16, killed by Slime Boss).
- Failure type: HAND_SELECT selection-count mismatch during a floor 16 boss state. The game already had one selected card with `max_cards=3`, but `SimpleAgent.handle_screen()` still requested three more cards. `CardSelectAction` rejected the action with `Too many cards selected (provided 3, max 2)`, and the batch loop repeatedly retried the same in-game screen without producing further `.run` records.
- Fix: HAND_SELECT now computes remaining required cards as `num_cards - len(selected_cards)`, avoids reselecting already-selected card objects, and confirms when the required count is already satisfied.
- Regression: `test_hand_select_only_selects_remaining_required_cards`.
- Verification after fix: focused regression `1 passed`; related card-select/agent/RL context tests `27 passed`; full pytest `1456 passed`.
- Next candidate: restart bounded validation after this commit and inspect remaining floor 16 boss deaths, especially RL EndTurnAction choices that force fallback takeover at low HP.

## Round 3 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `94d9c9e Respect remaining hand select count`.

- Failure type: HAND_SELECT no longer over-selected, but the queued optional confirm skipped HAND_SELECT confirmations when `confirm` was already available and `confirm_up` was false. Logs showed repeated HAND_SELECT callbacks and repeated `KEY action: key=CARD_*` selections followed by `Skipping optional card-select confirm`.
- Fix: allow stale optional card-select confirm on HAND_SELECT only when the `confirm` command is actually available, while preserving the existing guard that avoids confirming HAND_SELECT screens without confirm.
- Regression: `test_stale_card_select_confirm_fires_for_hand_select_with_confirm_available`.
- Verification after fix: focused regression `1 passed`; related card-select/agent tests `23 passed`; full pytest `1457 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation and inspect repeated Act 1 boss deaths, especially low-HP Hexaghost/Guardian turns where RL ends turn and fallback takeover chooses defensive or setup lines that still leave lethal current-turn damage.

## Round 4 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: `ce442d7 Confirm stale hand select screens`; same completed CommunicationMod batch was used for follow-up attribution before launching the next bounded validation.
- Baseline evidence: latest visible run from that batch, `1780458068.run`, died at floor 16 to Hexaghost with no `victory=true` in the batch.
- Failure type: healing-potion conservation risk. Logs showed `[POTION_GUARD] Using Blood Potion: incoming=16 hp=73/80 room=MonsterRoom monsters=3`, spending the run's only percent-heal potion in a high-HP normal fight. The later Hexaghost lethal turn ended at 9 HP before 24 incoming; keeping Blood Potion would have enabled survival with the already-selected 10 block line. Run potion usage fields were not treated as authoritative because logs showed the actual potion usage.
- Fix: healing/regen/fairy potion scoring now requires immediate lethal pressure, low-HP pressure, or moderate boss pressure. Damage, block, energy, and other potion classes keep the existing dangerous-combat thresholds.
- Regression: `test_potion_guard_saves_healing_potion_when_hp_is_high`; positive guard `test_potion_guard_uses_healing_potion_to_survive_lethal_turn`.
- Verification after fix: focused healing-potion regressions `2 passed`; `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` `36 passed`; full pytest `1459 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from `ce442d7` plus this potion fix and inspect remaining Act 1 boss deaths, especially low-HP boss turns where saved potion use, fallback takeover, and current-turn lethal scoring interact.

## Round 5 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `0888c65 Conserve healing potions for emergencies`; CommunicationMod still used Windows Python with `scripts/run_training_batch.py --eval --max-games 20 --phase conservative --restart-guidance`.

It completed 8 visible runs with no `victory=true`: deaths to Automaton twice, Hexaghost three times, Slime Boss once, 3 Sentries once, and Centurion and Healer once. The 9th game marker was `1780459838`, but no matching `.run` was written.

- Failure type: potion command pacing/state-stability risk. Runtime logs selected `PotionAction` repeatedly, including guard-selected Block/Elixir/Energy/Blessing/Smoke Bomb/Strength/Dexterity potions; one combat selected Smoke Bomb and then Blessing of the Forge on consecutive callbacks before any explicit state-stabilizing wait. All completed run files still reported `potions_floor_usage=[]`, so policy changes would be premature until the action execution boundary is stabilized.
- Fix: `PotionAction.execute()` now queues `WaitAction(timeout=1)` after potion use/discard commands, matching the stabilization pattern already used for shop potion purchases and forcing a state update opportunity before the next decision.
- Regression: `test_potion_action_execute_uses_get_real_potions_without_raw_potions` now asserts both the `potion use 0` command and the queued `WaitAction`.
- Verification after fix: focused regression red `1 failed` for missing queued wait, then green `1 passed`; `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` `36 passed`; full pytest `1459 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from `50ce48e Stabilize potion action updates` and verify whether completed run files now show non-empty potion usage when logs select PotionAction; if potion records remain empty, inspect sent command/effect confirmation at the CommunicationMod boundary before changing potion selection policy.

## Round 6 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `3d7164b Consolidate round five audit note`; full pytest baseline with disabled pytest cache and unique basetemp -> `1459 passed`.
- Validation: controlled CommunicationMod fresh run from `3d7164b`; `restart_sts_modded.ps1 -FreshRun` moved the leftover `IRONCLAD.autosave` from the manually stopped Round 5 batch before launch.
- Outcome before stopping for a focused fix: no Ironclad `victory=true`; completed new run `1780460697.run` died at floor 16 to Hexaghost and still reported `potions_floor_usage=[]`. Runtime logs from the same run selected `PotionAction` for Block Potion/Fear Potion; the following in-progress run also selected Dexterity Potion and Skill Potion before the batch was manually stopped for the focused fix.
- Failure type: potion command wait sequencing. The first potion-action stabilization fix queued `WaitAction(timeout=1)`, but `WaitAction` defaults to `requires_game_ready=False`, so the main loop could send `potion use ...` and `wait 1` back-to-back in the same action drain before the potion command response restored `game_is_ready`. That did not actually prove a post-potion state refresh.
- Fix: the wait queued by `PotionAction.execute()` is now ready-gated by setting `requires_game_ready=True`, so the coordinator waits for the potion command response before sending the stabilizing `wait 1`.
- Regression: `test_potion_action_execute_uses_get_real_potions_without_raw_potions` now asserts the queued `WaitAction` is `requires_game_ready=True`.
- Verification after fix: focused regression red `1 failed` on the non-ready-gated wait, then green `1 passed`; `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` `36 passed`; full pytest `1459 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from the ready-gated potion wait commit. If completed run files still show zero potion usage after logged PotionAction decisions, add direct command/effect instrumentation at `PotionAction.execute()` and CommunicationMod response handling before changing gameplay potion policy.

## Round 8 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `ff577d4 Conserve energy potions for pressure turns`; post-commit full pytest baseline -> `1461 passed`.
- Validation: controlled CommunicationMod fresh run with Windows Python. The batch was stopped manually after the selected crash was attributed, so it intentionally did not reach the 20-game bound. Before stopping, it wrote 3 AI markers and 2 completed `.run` files: `1780465389.run` died at floor 16 to Slime Boss and `1780465520.run` died at floor 25 to Snake Plant.
- Failure type: stale queued card action crash. During game #1 on floor 10, Elixir opened HAND_SELECT, stale card-select confirms produced invalid confirm errors, RL then played True Grit, and fallback planned Burning Pact from the latest hand. Before the queued `PlayCardAction` executed, the bound card UUID was no longer in `last_game_state.hand`, so `PlayCardAction.execute()` raised `Specified card for CardAction is not in hand`, aborting `play_one_game` and causing the batch loop to resume mid-run as game #2.
- Fix: when a UUID-bound `PlayCardAction` no longer finds that UUID in the latest hand, execution now logs a warning, sends `state`, and returns without sending a stale `play` command or raising. This lets the next state callback replan from the actual game state instead of failing the run.
- Regression: `test_play_card_action_requests_state_when_uuid_card_left_hand` reproduced the exception path red and now asserts the safe `state` request.
- Verification after fix: focused stale-card regression `1 passed`; related action/RL guard tests `40 passed`; full pytest `1462 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from this stale-card guard commit and check whether HAND_SELECT stale confirm errors still occur without aborting runs. If they recur but no longer crash, inspect the Elixir/HAND_SELECT selection loop as a separate focused target.

## Round 9 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `0208f87 Guard stale queued card actions`; post-commit full pytest baseline -> `1462 passed`.
- Validation: controlled CommunicationMod fresh run with Windows Python. The batch was stopped manually after the selected repeated-error issue was attributed. Before stopping, it completed 3 visible `.run` files with no `victory=true`: `1780466186.run` died at floor 16 to Slime Boss, `1780466237.run` died at floor 16 to The Guardian, and `1780466425.run` died at floor 33 to Automaton.
- Failure type: repeated command-error propagation. Stale `confirm` commands still occurred after HAND_SELECT or screen transitions, but the immediate damage was that the same `Invalid command: confirm` error was passed through `Coordinator.receive_game_state_update()` to `CombatRLAgent.handle_error()` on consecutive state updates. That incremented `rl_failure_count` three times for one stale-confirm episode, disabled RL for the rest of the run, and polluted the validation signal.
- Fix: coordinator command-error handling now deduplicates consecutive identical errors until a clean state arrives. It clears the queue, processes the first distinct error through `error_callback`, suppresses repeated identical errors, clears `last_error`, and requests `state` when no recovery action is queued.
- Regression: `test_repeated_command_error_is_handled_once_and_resyncs_state` reproduced two consecutive `Invalid command: confirm` messages red and now asserts one callback plus two state resyncs.
- Verification after fix: focused repeated-error regression `1 passed`; related coordinator/card/action tests `10 passed`; full pytest `1463 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from this coordinator error-dedupe commit. If stale confirms still occur but no longer disable RL, inspect the source of repeated HAND_SELECT confirms; if no protocol errors recur, return to Act 1 boss failure attribution.

## Round 10 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `5300275 Deduplicate repeated command errors`; post-commit full pytest baseline -> `1463 passed`.
- Validation: controlled CommunicationMod fresh run with Windows Python. The batch was stopped manually after the remaining HAND_SELECT stale-confirm source was attributed. Before stopping, it completed 7 visible `.run` files with no `victory=true`; all seven died at floor 16 to Act 1 bosses, dominated by Slime Boss.
- Failure type: HAND_SELECT command pacing. The coordinator dedupe fix worked: repeated `Invalid command: confirm` responses were suppressed instead of three-striking RL. The source still remained: `CardSelectAction` queued multiple HAND_SELECT `KeyAction`s and an optional stale confirm, all with `requires_game_ready=False`, so a single callback could send several card keys and `confirm` before any state response reflected the selections. That produced repeated stale confirm errors in game #8.
- Fix: `KeyAction` now supports explicit ready gating and response waiting. `CardSelectAction` uses ready-gated, response-waiting key commands for HAND_SELECT only, and the final optional HAND_SELECT confirm is also ready-gated. GRID selection keeps its existing immediate stale-confirm behavior.
- Regression: `test_hand_select_card_select_waits_between_keys_and_confirm` reproduced the old immediate HAND_SELECT queue red and now asserts key actions plus final confirm require game readiness, with HAND_SELECT keys waiting for responses.
- Verification after fix: focused HAND_SELECT queue regression `1 passed`; related card/coordinator/action tests `11 passed`; full pytest `1464 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from this HAND_SELECT pacing commit. If protocol errors stop, attribute the repeated Act 1 boss deaths; if HAND_SELECT confirm errors persist, inspect whether `SimpleAgent.handle_screen()` is reselecting too many cards before selected-card state updates arrive.

## Round 12 - 2026-06-03

- Preflight: git clean at `23ef5de Skip automatic Elixir guard use`; post-commit full pytest baseline with isolated repo-local basetemp -> `1465 passed`.
- Validation: CommunicationMod stayed on Windows Python with `scripts/run_training_batch.py --eval --max-games 20 --phase conservative --restart-guidance`. The first fresh launch reached the Slay the Spire main menu but did not start a run; process/log checks and offline model-load probes indicated a one-off CommunicationMod/pipe startup deadlock, not a code model-load blocker. A controlled restart then ran the bounded batch to `Max games reached (20); exiting.`
- Outcome: no Ironclad `victory=true`; the restarted batch wrote 20 AI markers and 18 visible completed `.run` files. The best visible run was `1780475627.run`, floor 50, died to Donu and Deca. Other visible deaths included repeated Act 1 bosses, Act 2 hallway/elites, and Act 2 bosses.
- Failure type: unsafe automatic utility/choice potion use on safe boss turns. Runtime logs showed repeated `POTION_GUARD` uses of `Attack Potion`, `Gambler's Brew`, `Blessing of the Forge`, and `Distilled Chaos` on `MonsterRoomBoss` turns with `incoming=0` or only `incoming=5`. These potions can open card-choice or hand-select-like decision screens or consume a one-time burst resource, so boss-room presence alone is not a sufficient danger signal. Matching logs also showed the same potion family being useful under `incoming>=18` normal combat pressure.
- Fix: potion guard scoring now treats utility/choice effects (`card_choice_*`, `discard_draw`, `play_top_cards`, `upgrade_hand`, `duplicate_next_card`, `return_discard_card`, and similar generated-card utility effects) as current-pressure tools. They no longer get a positive automatic score merely because the room is elite or boss, but they remain eligible when current incoming damage is at least 18.
- Regression: `test_potion_guard_saves_utility_choice_potion_on_safe_boss_turn` reproduced the boss-opening `Attack Potion` use red; `test_potion_guard_uses_utility_choice_potion_under_current_turn_pressure` preserves the high-pressure positive path.
- Verification after fix: focused utility-choice tests `2 passed`; `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` `41 passed`; full pytest with disabled cache provider and repo-local basetemp passed `1467 passed`.
- Next candidate: rerun bounded validation from this commit. If potion noise drops, inspect dominant remaining boss failures, especially floor 50 Donu/Deca and repeated Act 1 boss deaths where RL opens with `EndTurnAction` and fallback takeover must carry the whole turn.

- Fix: `CombatRLAgent` now detects a half-dead, non-gone monster in the finished-combat transition branch. If CommunicationMod exposes the `end` command, it returns a stamped `EndTurnAction`; otherwise it keeps the existing `WaitAction`. Ordinary dead-monster reward transitions still wait.
- Regression: `test_half_dead_revive_transition_ends_turn_instead_of_waiting` reproduces the half-dead Awakened One state. The existing `test_finished_combat_stale_state_waits_instead_of_playing_card` remains the guardrail for normal stale post-combat states.
- Verification after fix: focused regression red `1 failed`, then green alongside the normal post-combat guard `2 passed`; `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` passed `69 passed`; full pytest with disabled cache provider and repo-local basetemp passed `1638 passed`.

Player HP/block/energy diffs are still compared normally, so non-monster mistakes remain reportable.

## Round 113 - 2026-06-07

- Preflight: the current tracked worktree was clean at `9f8774b Model end-turn thorns attack ordering` before the next clean batch attempt. CommunicationMod still used Windows Python and the 5-game eval clean-trace command. A controlled `-FreshRun` relaunched ModTheSpire and entered callbacks, but the batch stopped during game 1 before a complete 5-run distribution could be trusted.
- Live blocker evidence: at floor 14 the agent used `Smoke Bomb`, then while CommunicationMod still reported a stale `screen=NONE`, `in_combat=True` combat frame, the coordinator called the combat agent again and sent two `PlayCardAction` commands for `Defend`. The run then reached a rest site, sent `RestAction`, stopped receiving state updates, and the visible ModTheSpire crash window showed a Java `NullPointerException` in `UseCardAction.update`. The trace contained the expected symptom row for a stale `Defend` play after the escaped combat: monster fields were `actual=null`, expected still in-combat, and player block/HP/energy were mismatched.
- Failure type: this was an operational/protocol synchronization blocker rather than a clean sim oracle class. `PotionAction` already queued one ready-gated `WaitAction`, and the coordinator already inserted one extra combat-action settle wait, but the settle flag was single-use. Escape potions such as `Smoke Bomb` can asynchronously leave combat after multiple stale `NONE/in_combat` state frames, so the single-use settle allowed a stale combat callback to run before the room transition was visible.
- Fix: `PotionAction` now marks escape potions (`effect_type="escape"` or `Smoke Bomb` identifiers) as requiring combat-room transition settlement. `Coordinator` preserves a separate room-transition settle flag for those terminal combat actions and keeps queuing short waits while the current state is still the stale `NONE/in_combat` frame. It clears the flag once the state leaves that stale combat frame, while ordinary combat actions still use the existing single extra settle wait.
- Regression: `test_escape_potion_waits_until_stale_combat_frame_clears` reproduces the live path by simulating `Smoke Bomb` followed by repeated stale `NONE/in_combat` updates and asserts the combat callback is not called. The existing `test_stale_none_combat_frame_after_play_waits_instead_of_calling_agent` keeps the non-terminal play-card settle behavior covered.
- Verification after fix: the focused escape-potion regression was red (`1 failed, 8 deselected`), then the focused escape/stale subset passed `2 passed, 7 deselected`; adjacent coordinator/potion/play-card action tests passed `21 passed`; full pytest with disabled cache provider and repo-local basetemp passed `1705 passed`.
- Next candidate: commit this synchronization fix, restart the crashed game with `-FreshRun`, and rerun a full clean 5-game batch from the new commit. Only after a completed fresh distribution should the next sim oracle issue be selected; the prior known candidate remains Combust end-turn damage triggering slime split before the slime action, but it must be revalidated in the new trace.

- Failure type: this was a mechanics sync gap, not evidence that the RL policy itself chose a bad line. `Entangled` blocks attack cards played by card effects such as `Havoc`; the top attack is still consumed/exhausted by Havoc, so exhaust-triggered effects such as Feel No Pain remain valid while attack play effects such as damage, Rage, Ornamental Fan, and Nunchaku should not fire.

Ironclad fallback block scoring and RL survival/Havoc guards also stop counting Ornamental Fan or deterministic top-attack value from a blocked top attack.
- Regression: new focused regressions cover the divergence oracle, fast simulator, lethal detector, Ironclad fallback Fan-block estimate, RL survival block estimate, and RL Havoc guard: `test_havoc_top_attack_is_blocked_by_entangled`, `test_lethal_detector_ignores_havoc_top_attack_when_entangled`, `test_ironclad_fallback_ignores_havoc_top_attack_fan_block_when_entangled`, `test_survival_guard_ignores_havoc_top_attack_fan_block_when_entangled`, and `test_havoc_guard_does_not_allow_visible_top_attack_when_entangled`.
- Verification after fix: the first three regressions were red (`3 failed`) before implementation, then green (`3 passed`); the fallback/RL additions were also red (`3 failed`) before their sync and green afterward (`6 passed` across the full focused set). Adjacent subsets passed: `tests/test_sim_divergence.py -k "havoc or Entangled or entangled"` returned `8 passed`, `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py -k "havoc or Entangled or entangled"` returned `10 passed`, `tests/test_ironclad_combat_guards.py -k "havoc or Entangled or entangled"` returned `18 passed`, and `tests/test_timing_planner_guards.py -k "havoc or Entangled or entangled"` returned `2 passed`. Touched test files passed `218`, `621`, and `97` tests respectively. Full pytest with disabled cache provider and repo-local basetemp passed `1928 passed`.

- A-cluster 2: the floor-18 `Havoc` row had visible draw-pile top `Second Wind`. Live consumed Havoc, played Second Wind from the draw pile, exhausted the two non-attack cards remaining after Havoc left hand, and gained `10` block. The old oracle consumed the top card but did not model Second Wind's hand-exhaust block when played by Havoc/top-card effect.
- Fix 2: Havoc/top-card effect settlement now applies Second Wind block using the non-attack cards still in hand after excluding the Havoc source card. This keeps the change scoped to deterministic visible top-card Second Wind behavior.
- B/C leftovers: the Calipers/thief escape player-block rows and the Mercury Hourglass/Collector end-turn HP rows crossed combat-exit, turn-start, death, or monster-action sequencing boundaries. The `Havoc+`/`CardSelectAction` Malleable pair is a candidate for future review, but this batch had only one pair and it depends on an asynchronous settlement boundary, so it was not fixed without recurrence.
- Regression: `test_hemokinesis_lethal_self_damage_prevents_attack_damage` reproduces the fresh Hemokinesis lethal self-damage row. `test_havoc_uses_known_draw_pile_top_second_wind_block` reproduces the fresh Havoc top Second Wind block row.

The same validation now backs the main RL combat `PlayCardAction` validity check, and non-end-turn fallback skips stale unplayable fallback cards before trying the first playable safe card.

This overcorrected the Round 170 fix by preventing invalid commands but throwing away repairable attacks.
- A-cluster: continuing fallback takeover actions can be stale after a prior guard replacement. The Round 170 validator correctly prevented sending invalid `PlayCardAction`s, but the direct takeover branch ended the turn immediately instead of clearing the stale takeover and choosing a current safe playable card. That explains the live batch's repeated lost attacks and early floor-10 to floor-16 deaths without reopening the already-fixed invalid-command bug.
- Fix: when a continuing takeover fallback action is not playable in the current live state, `CombatRLAgent` now clears `_fallback_turn_key` and asks `_first_playable_card_action` for a safe current replacement before ending the turn. The replacement keeps the existing self-lethal, pressure-unsafe HP-loss, and low-HP HP-loss filler filters. If no current safe card exists, the guard still ends the turn, preserving the 0-energy invalid-card protection.

The old code returned `EndTurnAction`; the fixed code repairs it to `PlayCardAction(card_index=0, target_index=0)` and clears `_fallback_turn_key`. The existing `test_energy_guard_takeover_suppresses_unpayable_cached_card` still verifies that a 1-cost `Defend` at 0 energy ends the turn instead of sending an invalid command.
- Verification after fix: the new regression was red before implementation (`1 failed`) and green after (`1 passed`). Focused takeover regressions passed (`3 passed`), `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` passed (`114 passed`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`1971 passed in 61.21s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean 5-game batch from this repair and inspect fresh outcomes. - A-cluster: on floor 4 turn 3 against an `Acid Slime (M)` at `7` HP, the player had `73/80` HP, `2` energy, and hand `Defend, Strike, Strike, Strike`. The fallback combat heuristic repeatedly detected lethal and built a one-card lethal sequence, but returned it as a card-object `PlayCardAction` with `card_index=-1` and no current target. When a `PlayCardAction` has no valid `card_index`, it maps the action's card object back to the current hand by object identity, UUID, or normalized card id/name with upgrade count preservation, then fills or preserves a live target as needed. If the repaired action is legal in the current combat state, both initial energy-guard fallback and continuing takeover use that same intended card instead of falling through to the first playable filler.

The old code chose `Defend`; the fixed code chooses `PlayCardAction(card_index=1, target_index=0)` in both paths.
- Verification after fix: the continuing-takeover regression was red before implementation (`1 failed`, selected `card_index=0`) and green after (`1 passed`). The initial-fallback regression then exposed the remaining gap and was red before the second patch (`1 failed`, selected `card_index=0`). After wiring the same repair into `_get_non_end_turn_fallback`, both card-object regressions passed (`2 passed`), focused energy-guard fallback/takeover repair tests passed (`6 passed, 121 deselected`), `tests/test_combat_rl_guards.py` passed (`127 passed`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`1989 passed in 61.00s`).

- Route/reward validation: the unsupported Heavy Blade same-screen reward issue did not recur in the same form: `1781282144.run` picked `Spot Weakness` after an early Heavy Blade, and the later Act 1 boss deaths were not driven by the exact Anger/Spot Weakness screens from the prior round. - A-cluster: conservative routing only represented elite avoidance as a large negative node reward. That still left the route planner comparing path reward totals as the primary objective, so a route containing an optional elite could win when downstream rewards looked better. Map logs also showed unreachable sentinel reward propagation from non-current branches during replans, which made route diagnostics noisy and could contaminate parent selection when no reachable complete route was available.
- Fix: `SimpleAgent.generate_map_route` now treats conservative routing as a lexicographic objective: minimize future elite count first, then maximize reward among routes with the same elite count. Replans seed the current node with zero future elites, so already-entered nodes are not counted retroactively. The DP now also skips unreachable sentinel nodes instead of propagating fake totals through unrelated branches.
- Regression: `test_conservative_route_prefers_complete_zero_elite_path_over_reward_bait` constructs a map with a complete zero-elite path and a higher-reward path through an elite. The old code selected the elite branch; the fixed route planner selects the zero-elite branch.

Logs showed `gold=331` then `gold=256` after purge, followed by `[SHOP_SCREEN] Purchase already made in this shop, exiting`; later Act 2 shops repeated the same one-transaction behavior with hundreds of gold remaining. This made shops behave like purge-only nodes even when the deck needed Act 2 survival help.
- Fix: `SimpleAgent` now records a shop state signature when it starts a transaction. While the state is unchanged it keeps the existing post-purchase wait/exit guard to avoid duplicate clicks; once gold, purge availability, or shop item counts change, it clears the pending purchase state and continues evaluating cards, relics, and potions in the same shop. Entering or leaving shop rooms resets the signature.
- Regression: `test_shop_screen_continues_buying_after_purge_state_updates` reproduces the live shop pattern with `250` gold, purge available, a removable Strike, and an affordable `Offering`. The old code returned a post-purchase `WaitAction` after the purge state update; the fixed code buys `Offering`.
- Verification after fix: the new regression was red before implementation (`1 failed`, returned `WaitAction`) and green after. `tests/test_shop_screen_guards.py` passed (`14 passed`), adjacent shop/action suites passed (`88 passed`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2012 passed in 65.76s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean 5-game batch from this shop-continuation fix. Validate that A0 shops no longer stop after purge when meaningful purchases remain. If shops improve but Act 1 boss deaths persist, inspect the next fresh route/card/event evidence, especially optional elite exposure and concrete event HP trades rather than the contaminated A17 sample.

Live routing logs showed conservative mode was active and elite nodes were heavily penalized (`priority=-5010`), yet the chosen path still routed `0:M -> 1:M -> 2:M -> 3:M -> 4:? -> 5:E`. The root cause was that the DP minimized total elite count, then broke equal-elite routes by early Act 1 monster count and reward. When every complete path still included an elite, it had no tie-break for delaying the first elite until after a rest or more preparation.
- Fix: conservative map routing now tracks the first elite floor along each DP path. It still prefers fewer total elites first; among equal-elite paths, it prefers the route whose first elite occurs later before applying early normal-fight or reward tie-breaks. This preserves the zero-elite preference and normal-fight density rules while avoiding an unnecessary floor-6 elite when a later forced elite route exists.
- Regression: `test_conservative_route_delays_forced_act1_elite_until_after_rest` builds two one-elite Act 1 routes: a higher early-fight branch that reaches an elite before any rest, and a lower-reward branch that reaches a rest before a later elite. The old code failed red by choosing the early elite branch.
- Verification after fix: the new regression failed red as expected (`1 failed`, chose the early branch) and passed after implementation (`1 passed`). `tests/test_map_routing_safety.py` passed (`20 passed in 0.50s`), `tests/test_map_routing_safety.py tests/test_ironclad_act1_strategy.py` passed (`29 passed in 0.55s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2039 passed in 69.23s`).

The target set is narrow: starter Strike/Defend cards and curses such as `Writhe`, `Injury`, `Pain`, `Parasite`, `Normality`, `Curse of the Bell`, and related curse names. If no target exists, the shop logs that paid purge is skipped and exits or considers other purchases; GRID removal behavior for already-entered purge/transform screens is left unchanged.

The bug is narrower: once the deck already has `Anger`, another copy should not beat a real strength enabler, key block card, or high-value utility card on the same screen. The reward scorer had duplicate protection for `Havoc`, but no corresponding duplicate-`Anger` guard.
- Fix: Act 1 reward scoring now tracks `anger_count`. If the deck already has `Anger` and the same reward screen contains a better upgrade/utility option (`Inflame`, `Spot Weakness`, `Feed`, survival block, `Armaments`, `Battle Trance`, `Offering`, or `Shockwave`), duplicate `Anger` is capped at a low score. The existing behavior where `Anger` beats unsupported `Heavy Blade` is preserved.
- Regression: `test_ironclad_strategy_prefers_inflame_over_second_anger_before_guardian` reproduces the floor-14 Guardian-prep screen from `1781366026.run`: `Anger`, `Inflame`, and `Flex` against a deck that already has `Anger+`. The old code failed red by selecting `Anger`.
- Verification after fix: the new regression failed red as expected (`1 failed`, selected `Anger`) and passed after implementation alongside the existing positive Anger guard (`2 passed`). `tests/test_ironclad_card_reward_guards.py` passed (`52 passed in 1.95s`), the focused reward/shop/event suite passed (`100 passed in 2.77s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2044 passed in 70.86s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean validation batch from this duplicate-`Anger` reward fix. Watch whether Act 1 reward screens now choose `Inflame`/block/utility over second `Anger`, whether shops keep avoiding duplicate low-impact buys, whether early Act 1 deaths shift toward route or combat, and whether Collector/Automaton summon-order divergence becomes repeated enough for a focused simulator regression.

The event context was lost between the event screen and the GRID screen.
- Fix: choosing `Back to Basics` / `Elegance` now marks the next GRID selection as `remove`. GRID handling consumes that one-shot mode and uses the same starter-first removal ordering as purge/transform, then clears the mode. Neutral grids such as copy/obtain screens still pick the best card when no event mode is set.
- Regressions: `test_back_to_basics_elegance_marks_next_grid_as_removal` verifies the event step stores the one-shot GRID removal context. `test_back_to_basics_elegance_grid_removes_starter_instead_of_good_card` reproduces the live false-neutral GRID shape with `Fiend Fire`, `Strike_R`, and `Defend_R`; the old code failed red by selecting `Fiend Fire`.

Purge remains allowed before the first card purchase, preserving the existing remove-then-buy flow.

The log's beam candidates showed defensive mixed sequences such as `Shrug It Off` plus `Twin Strike`, but the returned sequence was single-card `Immolate`. Because `_score_sequence` only applied a current-turn penalty when unblocked damage was lethal, it let 21 damage from `Immolate` outweigh taking 16 nonlethal damage at 34% HP. The run fell to 11 HP after turn 1 and died on turn 4.
- Fix: added a low-HP current HP-loss penalty to Ironclad combat scoring. When the sequence does not kill all monsters, current-turn unblocked damage is nonzero, and current or post-hit HP is in a low-margin range, `_score_sequence` now penalizes that HP loss with `W_DEATHRISK`, scaling higher at very low HP. This keeps lethal sequences untouched while making block-plus-damage beat pure damage when the pure damage line leaves the run near death.
- Regression: `test_beam_search_returns_top_low_hp_block_candidate_for_snake_plant` reproduces the live Snake Plant turn after the first `Defend`: 27 HP, 5 block, 2 energy, 21 incoming, and the `Immolate` / `Headbutt` / `Shrug It Off` / `Twin Strike` hand. - A-cluster: the deepest run `1781419821.run` reached the Act 1 boss reward screen with `Offering` / `Reaper` / `Fiend Fire`. `ai_debug.log` showed `Offering -> skip (Doesn't fit strength archetype)`, `Reaper -> take (Good card (score: 95))`, and `Fiend Fire -> skip (Doesn't fit strength archetype)`, then picked `Reaper`. The same run later skipped a floor-31 `Immolate` / `Second Wind+` / `Flex` screen because `Immolate` and `Second Wind+` were filtered as not fitting the strength archetype. This hard archetype gate prevented evaluator scoring from considering universal premium cards that solve immediate damage and deck cycling.
- Fix: added a narrow `ARCHETYPE_INDEPENDENT_PREMIUM` allowlist in `IroncladArchetypeManager` for `Offering`, `Fiend Fire`, and `Immolate`. These cards now pass the archetype consistency gate while the existing score, deck-size, HP-risk, and Act 1 support checks still decide whether to take them.
- Regressions: `test_ironclad_strategy_allows_fiend_fire_in_strength_archetype` reproduces the live strength-archetype boss reward context and failed red with `Doesn't fit strength archetype`. `test_strength_archetype_large_deck_takes_immolate_before_act2_boss` reproduces the live floor-31 `Immolate` / `Second Wind+` / `Flex` screen and failed red by returning `CancelAction`.
- Verification after fix: both regressions passed after implementation (`2 passed`). `tests/test_ironclad_card_reward_guards.py` passed (`76 passed in 3.10s`), the combined route/reward/shop/event/rest/combat/sim suite passed (`1435 passed in 33.16s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2094 passed in 75.43s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean validation batch from this archetype-premium reward fix. Continue watching Reaper versus frontload rare rewards, skipped premium Act 2 attacks, Slime Boss/Guardian preparation, ignored `Golden Idol`, event HP trades, and whether the lone floor-29 HP trace recurs with cleaner mechanics evidence.

## Round 258 - 2026-06-14

- Preflight: after `7201cd21 Allow premium rewards across archetypes`, a clean validation batch was launched with `scripts\restart_sts_modded.ps1 -FreshRun`. Fresh AI markers were `1781421504`, `1781421608`, `1781422202`, `1781422353`, and `1781422469`; corresponding A0 Ironclad run files were `1781421500.run`, `1781421604.run`, `1781422199.run`, `1781422349.run`, and `1781422465.run`. All had `victory=false`.
- Outcomes and prior-fix validation: the batch produced Hexaghost floor 16, Hexaghost floor 16, Awakened One floor 50, Snake Plant floor 28, and Snecko floor 22. The archetype-independent premium fix had a positive signal in the deep run: `1781422199.run` included `Offering+1`, `Impervious+1`, `Bludgeon`, and stable block cards, and reached the Act 3 boss. No `victory=true` run was produced.
- Live recovery evidence: during `1781422199.run`, the visible game reached the Awakened One revive transition and stopped advancing without ending the turn. Screenshot `debug_screenshots/sts_screen_20260614_152902_761.png` showed floor 50, player alive at `21/85`, Awakened One in revive posture, `End Turn` available, and playable hand cards. `ai_debug.log` repeatedly emitted `[POST_COMBAT_GUARD] in_combat still true but no monsters alive; waiting for reward transition` and returned `WaitAction`. The decision trace immediately before the loop showed Awakened One serialized as `gone=true`, `half_dead=true`, `hp=0`, `Intent.UNKNOWN`.
- Recovery action: without restarting the game, the window was activated and the visible `End Turn` button was clicked once. The fight resumed into Awakened One phase two, then later ended normally with `1781422199.run` at `victory=false`, floor 50, `killed_by=Awakened One`. This confirmed the stuck state needed an end-turn action, not a restart or reward-screen wait.
- A-cluster: `_should_end_reviving_combat_transition` only treated `half_dead` monsters as revive transitions when `is_gone` was false. CommunicationMod can serialize Awakened One's revive animation as both `is_gone=true` and `half_dead=true`; because `_alive_monsters()` excludes both fields, the post-combat guard mistook the live revive transition for a stale finished combat and waited forever.
- Fix: the revive-transition guard now treats any `half_dead` monster as a reason to send `EndTurnAction` when the `end` command is available, even if `is_gone` is also true. The normal stale finished-combat path still waits when all monsters are merely dead/gone and no `half_dead` revive marker is present.
- Regression: `test_gone_half_dead_awakened_one_revive_transition_ends_turn` reproduces the live state with a dead Cultist and an Awakened One that is `is_gone=true` plus `half_dead=true`. The old code failed red by returning `WaitAction`.
- Verification after fix: the new regression failed red as expected (`1 failed`, returned `WaitAction`) and passed with the existing half-dead revive and stale finished-combat guards (`3 passed`). The focused combat/half-dead/action-space suite passed (`800 passed in 23.15s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2095 passed in 72.70s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so the next clean batch should restart/reload the fixed code before validation. Continue watching Act 3 boss survivability, Act 2 hallway deaths, Snecko/Fusion Hammer boss relic context, early Hexaghost preparation, ignored `Golden Idol`, event HP trades, and shop/card-reward decisions now that a deep run has reached Awakened One.

## Round 259 - 2026-06-14

- Preflight: after `718d9616 Handle gone half-dead revive transitions`, a clean validation batch was launched with `scripts\restart_sts_modded.ps1 -FreshRun` to load the Awakened One revive fix. Fresh AI markers were `1781423003`, `1781423098`, `1781423191`, `1781423295`, and `1781423422`; corresponding A0 Ironclad run files were `1781422999.run`, `1781423094.run`, `1781423187.run`, `1781423291.run`, and `1781423418.run`. All had `victory=false`, and the batch reached `Max games reached (5); exiting.` No CommunicationMod traceback was present.
- Outcomes and prior-fix validation: the batch produced Guardian floor 16, Guardian floor 16, Guardian floor 16, 3 Byrds floor 20 after beating Slime Boss, and Slime Boss floor 16. The prior Awakened One revive issue did not replay because no fresh run reached Awakened One; the batch instead exposed an Act 1 Guardian simulator mechanics gap.
- Trace distribution: fresh sim-divergence after cutoff `1781422900` had two rows. The strong row was floor 16, turn 4, `guardian_sharp_hide_reflection` after playing `Sever Soul` into Guardian while `Double Tap` power was active: live Guardian HP fell from `192` to `160` and player HP fell from `80` to `74`, while the simulator expected player HP `77`. The weak row was a floor-14 end-turn block drift (`actual=5`, `expected=0`) and remains a watch item.
- Route, reward, shop, event, and campfire check: routes still showed repeated Act 1 boss pressure plus one Act 2 3 Byrds death, but no map-selection rule beat the exact mechanics trace. Rewards included several watch items such as early `Rage`, duplicate utility, `Demon Form` over `Feed`, and Act 1 boss prep gaps; no reward screen was cleaner than the selected trace. Shops stayed bounded to starter purges plus controlled buys such as `Pantograph`, `Regal Pillow`, `Shrug It Off`, and `Darkstone Periapt`. Events included `World of Goop`, `WeMeetAgain`, `Golden Idol`, `Mushrooms`, `Living Wall`, `Big Fish`, `Golden Wing`, `Drug Dealer`, and `Bonfire Elementals`; event choices remain watch items but were weaker than the direct Guardian divergence. Campfires included sensible rests before bosses plus upgrades such as `Shockwave`, `Battle Trance`, `Pommel Strike`, and `Feed`.
- A-cluster: `_expected_after_action` already calculated `attack_play_count` and looped attack damage for `Double Tap`, so `Sever Soul` dealt damage twice. However, after the attack loop it only applied `_sharp_hide_reflection_damage` once. Guardian's `Sharp Hide` triggers per attack card play, not per damage hit; a doubled attack therefore reflects twice, while ordinary multi-hit attacks still reflect once.
- Fix: multiply Guardian `Sharp Hide` reflection by `max(1, attack_play_count)` for both single-target and all-enemy direct attack handling. This covers `Double Tap`, `Duplication`, and `Necronomicon` replay paths without multiplying by hit count.
- Regression: `test_double_tap_attack_triggers_guardian_sharp_hide_twice` reproduces the fresh Guardian trace with `Sever Soul`, `Double Tap`, Guardian `Sharp Hide` amount `3`, live player HP `74`, and Guardian HP `160`. The old code failed red by emitting a divergence because it expected player HP `77`.
- Verification after fix: the new regression failed red as expected (`1 failed`) and passed after implementation (`1 passed`). Guardian `Sharp Hide` adjacent tests passed (`7 passed, 253 deselected`), `tests/test_sim_divergence.py` passed (`260 passed in 13.68s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2096 passed in 77.66s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean validation batch from this Guardian reflection fix. Continue watching repeated Act 1 boss prep, the weak floor-14 block drift, Act 2 3 Byrds survival, `Demon Form` over `Feed`, early `Rage`/utility rewards, event HP/gold trades, and whether route safety needs a direct guard after mechanics traces quiet down.

All were AI-marked with `victory=false`. CommunicationMod error tail contained only normal training-batch startup and maintenance text.
- Outcomes and prior-fix validation: the batch produced Guardian floor 16, Snecko floor 25 after beating Slime Boss, Collector floor 33 after beating Slime Boss, Hexaghost floor 16, and Champ floor 33 after beating Slime Boss. The prior Book of Stabbing `Regenerate` issue did not recur; fresh divergence instead exposed a player defensive relic gap around Torii.
- Trace distribution: fresh sim-divergence after cutoff `1781425500` had six rows: five `player_state_mismatch` rows and one weaker `monster_state_mismatch` row. The strong cluster was the first run's Torii line: with player block `5`, Torii, and incoming small unblocked attack damage from Slaver/Guardian turns, live HP stayed two to five points higher than expected. Example: floor 11 Red Slaver hit for `8` through `5` block, so live HP went `31 -> 30`, while the simulator expected `31 -> 28`.
- Route, reward, shop, event, and campfire check: route pressure remained visible in two Act 2 boss deaths, one Act 2 hallway death, and two Act 1 boss deaths, but no map-selection rule beat the exact HP-loss evidence. Rewards had watch items such as `Mark of Pain` over `Pandora's Box`/`Astrolabe`, `Velvet Choker`, `Sozu` over `Runic Pyramid`/`Astrolabe`, repeated `Clothesline`, `Rampage`, and `Havoc`, but no repeated reward filter issue cleaner than Torii. Shops stayed bounded to purges plus controlled buys like `Anger`, `Inflame`, `Shrug It Off`, `Immolate`, `Feel No Pain`, and a `Block Potion`. Events included `Scrap Ooze`, `The Cleric`, `Liars Game`, `Forgotten Altar`, `Designer`, `Duplicator`, `Golden Wing`, `The Library`, `Addict`, `Drug Dealer`, and `Colosseum`; `The Library` heal/sleep and HP-cost events remain watch items but were weaker than the direct mechanics cluster. Campfires included many rests before bosses and sensible upgrades such as `Shockwave`, `Immolate`, `Spot Weakness`, `Dark Embrace`, and `Anger`.
- A-cluster: `_damage_player` used the generic HP-loss reducer for all damage after block, so enemy attacks applied Intangible, Tungsten Rod, Buffer, and revives but not Torii. Torii only applies to small unblocked attack damage, so this should not be folded into generic `_lose_player_hp` paths such as Bloodletting, Pain, Combust, or status HP loss. The missing Torii reduction made the simulator predict lethal or lower HP during the same Guardian fight where the run survived several extra turns.
- Fix: added attack-damage-specific HP-loss reducers in both `sim_divergence.py` and `heuristics/simulation.py`. Enemy attack damage after block now applies Intangible, Torii for `2..5` unblocked attack damage, Tungsten Rod, and Buffer in order. Generic HP loss and non-attack damage remain unchanged, and fast-sim state keys now include `has_torii` so planner projections can distinguish Torii states.
- Regressions: `test_torii_reduces_small_unblocked_end_turn_attack_damage` reproduces the fresh Slaver trace with Torii, `5` block, and `8` incoming attack damage. `test_torii_reduces_projected_small_unblocked_attack_damage` covers the planner projection path for the same `8` through `5` block scenario.
- Verification after fix: the two new regressions passed (`2 passed in 15.87s`). `tests/test_sim_divergence.py` passed (`262 passed in 12.41s`), and full pytest with disabled cache provider plus repo-local basetemp passed (`2099 passed in 67.13s`).
- Remaining candidates: no `victory=true` run yet, so rerun a clean validation batch from this Torii attack-damage fix. The prior Torii HP-loss cluster did not recur in fresh trace. Best floor was 33; no `victory=true` run was produced.
- Trace distribution: fresh sim-divergence after cutoff `1781427000` had one row: a floor-11 Looter `EndTurnAction` `player.block` mismatch while the monster escaped and combat was transitioning out. Live and expected both had the monster gone; only retained block differed. This was classified as B/C combat-finish boundary noise and was not selected for repair.
- Route, reward, shop, event, and campfire check: route pressure still showed Act 1 boss losses, early Act 2 hallway deaths, and two Champ losses, but no map rule beat the direct reward/relic/shop evidence. Rewards exposed a clean floor-30 skip in `1781427586.run`: `Combust+`, `Fiend Fire`, and `True Grit` were all skipped before a Champ death because the large-deck gate filtered `Fiend Fire` as not top-tier. Boss relics exposed `1781427839.run` picking `Ectoplasm` over `Pandora's Box` and `Astrolabe` after Hexaghost, then dying early Act 2 with gold locked. Shops mostly stayed bounded to purge plus controlled buys, but `1781428039.run` generated a stale `LeaveAction` after a card and potion purchase: by execution time the state had returned to `SHOP_ROOM` where `proceed` was available, making `leave` invalid before recovery. Events included Big Fish, Shining Light, Match and Keep, Golden Wing, Mushrooms, Living Wall, Liars Game, The Cleric, World of Goop, Nest, Upgrade Shrine, Forgotten Altar, Addict, Masked Bandits, and Fountain; no event choice beat the selected strategy/operation issues.
- A-clusters: first, `IroncladDeckStrategy.TIER_0_1_CARDS` omitted `Fiend Fire`, so decks at 20+ cards rejected it before evaluator scoring even though other Ironclad evaluators treat it as premium frontload/exhaust support. Second, Ironclad boss relic priority placed `Ectoplasm` ahead of `Astrolabe` and `Pandora's Box`, forcing a gold-locking energy relic over deck-transforming options. Third, `LeaveAction` handled stale `leave` commands only by requesting state; when a shop exit action lands on `SHOP_ROOM` with `proceed` available, it can safely complete the exit with `proceed` instead of creating an invalid command round-trip.
- Fixes: added `Fiend Fire` to the large-deck top-tier Ironclad card set; moved Ironclad `Ectoplasm` below `Pandora's Box` while leaving stronger higher-priority energy/relic options intact; and taught `LeaveAction.execute` to send `proceed` with the usual ready wait when the current state is already `SHOP_ROOM` and `proceed` is available.
- Regressions: `test_large_deck_takes_fiend_fire_before_act2_boss` reproduced the live `Combust` / `Fiend Fire` / `True Grit` Champ-prep skip and failed red with `CancelAction`. `test_ironclad_boss_relic_selection_prefers_pandoras_box_over_ectoplasm` and `test_ironclad_boss_relic_selection_prefers_astrolabe_over_ectoplasm` failed red by selecting `Ectoplasm`. `test_leave_action_uses_proceed_after_shop_screen_returns_to_room` failed red by sending `state` instead of `proceed`.
